So the algorithm is: For warp handling queries q0..q7: For each query index q (0..7), actual query = block_q_start + warp_id*8 + q For each key tile: 1. Find m_local = max of the 2 S values in this thread 3. m_cur = warpReduceMax(m_local) 4. m_new = max(m_old[q], m_cur) 5. exp_m_scale = exp(m_old[q] - m_new) 6. For thread's 2 keys: p0 = exp(S0 - m_new) p1 = exp(S1 - m_new) p_sum_local = p0 + p1 7. l_cur = warpReduceSum(p_sum_local) 8. l_new[q] = exp_m_scale * l_old[q] + l_cur 9. For di=0..3 (D dims handled by thread): O[q][di] *= exp_m_scale V0 = V_smem[key0, di_global] V1 = V_smem[key1, di_global] O[q][di] += p0 * V0 + p1 * V1 10. m_old[q] = m_new 11. l_old[q] = l_new[q] This is elegant and register-efficient. To avoid conflicts when multiple d's are accessed? Our K access is fixed d across warp, varying k. So bank = function of k. No inter-thread conflicts as analyzed if stored as [D][BK] with each thread accessing consecutive k. But to benefit from vectorized loads when transposing from global to shared: Global K is [S][D]. K_global[k*D + d]. If we load with __nv_bfloat162, we read K_global[k][d_pair]. To transpose into K_smem[d][k], writing becomes scattered. Alternatively, load K as [BK][D] (non-transposed) and then access transposed via bank-conflict aware indexing? If K_smem is [BK][D] (64 rows of 128 bf16), reading K[k][d] for fixed d, varying k: address = (k * D + d) * 2. Bank = (k * 128 + d) / 2 % 32 = (k * 64 + d/2) % 32 = d/2 % 32 (since 64 mod 32 = 0). All threads reading different k but same d hit the same bank! 32-way conflict! BAD. So K MUST be transposed to [D][BK] for reads. Loading K transposed: each thread loads a __nv_bfloat162 from global K[k][2*d_pair]. And writes to K_smem[2*d_pair][k] and K_smem[2*d_pair+1][k]? That's bad. Better: load K into smem as [D/2][BK] of __nv_bfloat162? No, that's still pairs of d for fixed k. Wait, we want K_smem[d][k] accessible. If we define K_smem as __nv_bfloat16 K_smem[128][66] with padding. Load: thread t loads from global. We can have each thread load a __nv_bfloat162 from K_global and scatter it. Actually, we can load cooperatively: For i = tid; i < BK * D / 2; i += NUM_THREADS: int k = i / (D/2); int d_pair = i % (D/2); __nv_bfloat162 val = K_global_ptr[...]; // Write to K_smem[2*d_pair][k] and K_smem[2*d_pair+1][k] // This requires splitting the pair, which is expensive. Alternative: Keep K_smem as [BK][D] but pad inner dim heavily so banks vary with k. If inner dim = 128 + pad. Let's pad to 136? No, as analyzed, bank for K[k][d] depends on k * inner_dim/2. We need inner_dim/2 to be odd. inner_dim = 132 bytes = 66 bf16. Then inner_dim/2 = 33 (odd). Address = (k * 66 + d) * 2. Bank = (k * 66 + d) / 2 % 32 = (k * 33 + d/2) % 32 = (k + d/2) % 32. For fixed d, thread t reading k=2t and 2t+1: banks = (2t + d/2) % 32 and (2t+1 + d/2) % 32. These are different banks and across the warp cover all banks. Same-thread accesses to different banks might conflict if the hardware can't dual-issue, but there are no cross-thread bank conflicts. This is good! So K_smem can be [BK][D_pad] of bf16, where D_pad = 66 bf16 elements. But careful: K_global is [S][D]. We load K_global[k_global][d] and store to K_smem[k_local][d_local]. With vectorized __nv_bfloat162 loads: load d_pair from global, store to K_smem[k][2*d_pair] and K_smem[k][2*d_pair+1]. The store is to consecutive d in K_smem row. These map to banks (k + d_pair) % 32 and (k + d_pair) % 32? Wait: K_smem[k][d] at address (k * 66 + d) * 2. For d=2*d_pair: bank = (k*33 + d_pair) % 32. For d=2*d_pair+1: bank = (k*33 + d_pair + 0.5) ... not integer. Actually with bf16: two elements at offset 2*d_pair and 2*d_pair+1 are in the same 4-byte bank word. Writing them separately might be inefficient. If we store as __nv_bfloat162 K_smem[BK][33] (33 pairs = 66 bf16). Then K_smem[k][d_pair] is at address (k * 33 + d_pair) * 4. Bank = (k * 33 + d_pair) % 32 = (k + d_pair) % 32. For fixed k consecutive d_pair: consecutive banks. Good. For fixed d_pair, varying k: banks differ by k. Good. So let's store K and V in smem as __nv_bfloat162 K_smem[BK][33]; where each row is one key (64 keys total), and each element is a pair of D dims. But we need K[k][d] meaning for key k, dim d. K_smem[k][d_pair] stores K[k][2*d_pair] and K[k][2*d_pair+1]. When reading in compute, for a specific d: - If d is even: read K_smem[k][d/2].x - If d is odd: read K_smem[k][d/2].y This is messy. And threads in a warp read the same d. If d is even, all threads read .x of pair d/2. Bank = (k + d/2) % 32. For fixed d/2, varying k: banks = k % 32 + const. Good, no cross-thread conflict! But all threads reading .x means they read the same half of the pair, which is fine. However, for vectorized loads during the transposition? We don't need transposition with this layout! K_smem is [BK][D_pairs], which is row-major per key. This matches global memory layout. Loading is just direct copy. And for the compute step (Q @ K^T), for fixed d, threads read K[k][d]. If d is even, all threads read .x from K_smem[k][d/2]. If d is odd, all read .y. Bank for K_smem[k][d_pair]: (k + d_pair) % 32. For fixed d_pair, different k -> different banks. Thread t reads k0 and k1. d_pair = d/2. Bank k0 = (k0 + d_pair) % 32, Bank k1 = (k1 + d_pair) % 32. These are different if k0 != k1. No cross-thread conflicts. But same thread reads two different banks. Fine. Great! So we can use non-transposed layout for K and V in smem, with inner dimension padded to an odd number of pairs (33 pairs for D=128=64 pairs). Actually D=128 means 64 pairs. Pad to 65 pairs? 65 mod 32 = 1. Bank = (k*65 + d_pair)%32 = (k + d_pair)%32. Good. Let's use inner dim = 65 for K_smem and V_smem. Q_smem: [BQ][65] of __nv_bfloat162. D=128=64 pairs. Inner dim 65. Bank = (q*65 + d_pair)%32 = (q + d_pair)%32. Good. Wait, Q_smem[q][d_pair]. When all threads read the same q and same d_pair (broadcast), they all hit the same bank. But it's a broadcast! Shared memory broadcasts are fast (all threads read same address). So that's actually optimal, not a conflict. When threads in a warp read the same q but different d_pairs (e.g., for vectorized computation), they hit different banks since d_pair varies. Good. For K_smem[k][d_pair]: when threads read different k but same d_pair, banks differ by k. Good. So the layouts: - Q_smem: __nv_bfloat162 Q_smem[64][65]; - K_smem: __nv_bfloat162 K_smem[64][65]; - V_smem: __nv_bfloat162 V_smem[64][65]; Wait, for V_smem, the access pattern in PV accumulation is: For each key k, we need V[k][d] for the same d across keys? No. O[qi][d] += p_k * V[k][d]. For each key k sequentially (or for thread's assigned keys): Thread reads V[k0][d] and V[k1][d] for its assigned d's. Wait, in my earlier design, each thread handles 4 D dimensions. For PV: O[qi][di] += p0 * V[k0][d_idx] + p1 * V[k1][d_idx]. For fixed d_idx, threads read V[k][d_idx] for different k. Bank for V[k][d_pair] where d_idx is in pair d_pair: V_smem[k][d_pair] stores V[k][2*d_pair] and V[k][2*d_pair+1]. If d_idx = 2*d_pair (even), all threads reading for different k read .x from V_smem[k][d_pair]. Bank = (k + d_pair) % 32. Thread t reads k0=2t, k1=2t+1. Banks: (2t + d_pair)%32 and (2t+1+d_pair)%32. Different banks, no cross-thread conflict. Perfect. If d_idx = 2*d_pair+1 (odd), read .y. Same banks. This layout works perfectly! Now, Q_smem: [BQ][65] of __nv_bfloat162. Each row q has 128 bf16 = 64 pairs. We pad to 65 pairs (260 bytes). Size: 64 * 65 * 4 = 16,640 bytes. K_smem: [BK][65] of __nv_bfloat162. BK=64. Size: 64 * 65 * 4 = 16,640 bytes. V_smem: [BK][65] of __nv_bfloat162. Size: 16,640 bytes. Total shared memory: 49,920 bytes. Well within limits. Shared memory loading: ```cpp #define D_PAIRS 64 #define SMEM_STRIDE_PAIRS 65 // Load Q for (int i = tid; i < BQ * SMEM_STRIDE_PAIRS; i += NUM_THREADS) { int q = i / SMEM_STRIDE_PAIRS; int d_pair = i % SMEM_STRIDE_PAIRS; if (d_pair < D_PAIRS) { int global_offset = b * Nq * S * D + hq * S * D + (q_start + q) * D + d_pair * 2; __nv_bfloat162 val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(Q + global_offset); Q_smem[q][d_pair] = val; } } ``` Wait, this is not fully coalesced if consecutive threads get consecutive d_pair but same q. Thread 0: offset for q=0, d_pair=0. Address = base + 0. Thread 1: q=0, d_pair=1. Address = base + 4. ... Thread 31: q=0, d_pair=31. Address = base + 124. These are consecutive 4-byte accesses. Coalesced! Yes. But what about boundary: d_pair < D_PAIRS (64). SMEM_STRIDE_PAIRS=65. The last element in each row (index 64) is padding. Threads with d_pair=64 skip write. This means some threads in a warp do nothing while others do, causing divergence? Only for the last thread out of 65. But 65 doesn't divide evenly. With 256 threads and BQ=64, we have 64*65=4160 elements. 4160/256 = 16.25. So the last few threads might straddle boundary. But the condition `if (d_pair < D_PAIRS)` handles it simply. A better load: iterate by total actual elements to avoid waste. `for (int i = tid; i < BQ * D_PAIRS; i += NUM_THREADS)` Then compute q = i / D_PAIRS, d_pair = i % D_PAIRS. Write to Q_smem[q][d_pair]. For K and V: same pattern but note global indexing for K, V uses hk. ```cpp int hk = hq / num_head_groups; int global_k_offset = b * Nkv * S * D + hk * S * D + k_start * D; ``` Algorithm details for compute: Warp w (0..7) handles queries q_base = w * 8 to w * 8 + 7. Within warp, lane = tid % 32. For each query qi_local (0..7): actual_q = q_base + qi_local. // But wait, if we unroll over 8 queries, register pressure might be high. // 8 queries * 2 keys * 1 float + 8 queries * 4 D * 1 float + 8 m/l stats... // S_reg: 8*2 = 16 floats. // O_reg: 8*4 = 32 floats. // m, l: 8*2 = 16 floats. // Total 64 floats = 256 bytes. If thread writes 4 consecutive D values, then for 32 threads, they write 128 values. Perfect coverage. But to have 32 threads each write 4 consecutive values, we can assign: d_base = (lane & 7) * 16 + (lane >> 3) * 2? That gives spacing. Wait, 32 threads * 4 dims = 128 dims. Then thread 0 writes d=0,1,2,3. Thread 1 writes d=4,5,6,7. When writing O[q][d], thread 0 writes at q*D+0. Thread 1 at q*D+4. Not fully coalesced within warp because each thread writes 8 bytes (4 bf16), but threads write to consecutive 8-byte chunks. That's coalesced! Actually, consecutive threads write to consecutive memory: thread 0: [0,8), thread 1: [8,16), etc. That's perfect coalescing. So d indices for thread lane: lane*4 + 0, 1, 2, 3. d_pair_base = lane * 2; // since each pair is 2 bf16 Each thread handles d_pair0 = lane*2 and d_pair1 = lane*2 + 1. For O_reg, we have O0, O1, O2, O3 corresponding to 4 consecutive D values. When reading V for PV accumulation: V[k][d] where d = lane*4 + offset (0..3). In V_smem[k][d_pair] where d_pair = d/2. For d = lane*4: d_pair = lane*2. Read V_smem[k][lane*2].x, .y for d+1. For d = lane*4+2: d_pair = lane*2+1. Read V_smem[k][lane*2+1].x, .y for d+3. Wait, but V_smem is [BK][65] of __nv_bfloat162. In QK^T, we iterate d from 0 to 127. For each d (or pair of d), load Q once per query. Since queries are in the same warp, and D is iterated, we can do: for (int d_pair = 0; d_pair < D_PAIRS; d_pair++) { __nv_bfloat162 q_vec = Q_smem[q][d_pair]; float q0 = __bfloat162float(q_vec.x); float q1 = __bfloat162float(q_vec.y); // But wait, each warp handles 8 queries. So for each d_pair, we add Q[qi][2dp]*K[k][2dp] + Q[qi][2dp+1]*K[k][2dp+1]. Exactly as above. This is great! We process D in pairs, reducing iterations by half. Let's write this kernel for real. ```cuda #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <cuda_bf16.h> #include <math_constants.h> // Use this or torch's own bf16 type. I'll assume __nv_bfloat16 is available. ``` Actually, for PyTorch inline loading, I can just write the CUDA source as a string. Let me draft the full model code. ```python import torch import torch.nn as nn import torch.nn.functional as F from torch.utils.cpp_extension import load_inline cuda_source = r''' #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <cuda_bf16.h> #include <math_constants.h> #include <cooperative_groups.h> using namespace cooperative_groups; #define D 128 #define BQ 64 #define BK 64 #define NUM_THREADS 256 #define D_PAIRS (D / 2) #define SMEM_STRIDE 65 // pairs per row in smem // Helper for bf162 -> float2 __device__ __forceinline__ float2 bf1622float2(__nv_bfloat162 val) { float2 r; r.x = __bfloat162float(val.x); r.y = __bfloat162float(val.y); return r; } __global__ void gqa_attn_kernel( const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ Q, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ K, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ V, __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ O, int B, int Nq, int Nkv, int S ) { int b = blockIdx.z; int hq = blockIdx.y; int q_tile = blockIdx.x; int num_head_groups = Nq / Nkv; int hk = hq / num_head_groups; int tid = threadIdx.x; int lane = tid & 31; int warp_id = tid >> 5; int q_local = warp_id; // each warp handles 8 queries? Wait, we said 8 warps * 8 queries = 64. // Actually earlier I said 8 warps each handle 8 queries. // Total queries = 64. 8 warps * 8 queries = 64. Yes. // But wait, NUM_THREADS=256=8 warps. Each warp handles 8 queries? // A warp has 32 threads. If it handles 8 queries, each query has 4 threads. // But I analyzed with each warp handling 8 queries and each thread handling 2 keys for all 8 queries. // 8 queries * 2 keys = 16 S values per thread. // Let's check if this fits: 32 threads * 2 keys = 64 keys. Perfect. // 8 queries in warp. Each thread computes S for its 2 keys across all 8 queries. // Actually, to simplify, let's fix: each warp handles 8 queries. // warp 0: queries 0..7 // warp 1: queries 8..15 // ... // warp 7: queries 56..63 int q_start_tile = q_tile * BQ; int q_start_warp = q_start_tile + warp_id * 8; // Shared memory extern __shared__ char smem[]; __nv_bfloat162* q_smem = (__nv_bfloat162*)smem; __nv_bfloat162* k_smem = q_smem + BQ * SMEM_STRIDE; __nv_bfloat162* v_smem = k_smem + BK * SMEM_STRIDE; // Load Q tile into smem (all 256 threads participate) // Q layout: [B, Nq, S, D] const int q_stride_batch = Nq * S * D; const int q_stride_head = S * D; int q_base_offset = b * q_stride_batch + hq * q_stride_head + q_start_tile * D; for (int i = tid; i < BQ * D_PAIRS; i += NUM_THREADS) { int q = i / D_PAIRS; int dp = i % D_PAIRS; int global_offset = q_base_offset + q * D + dp * 2; __nv_bfloat162 val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(Q + global_offset); q_smem[q * SMEM_STRIDE + dp] = val; } __syncthreads(); // Each thread in warp handles 2 keys: key0, key1 int key0 = lane * 2; int key1 = lane * 2 + 1; // Each thread handles 4 consecutive D dimensions for O int d0 = lane * 4; int d_pair0 = lane * 2; // covers d0, d0+1 int d_pair1 = lane * 2 + 1; // covers d0+2, d0+3 // Registers float o_reg[8][4]; // 8 queries, 4 D dims float m_reg[8]; float l_reg[8]; #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; qi++) { m_reg[qi] = -CUDART_INF_F; l_reg[qi] = 0.0f; #pragma unroll for (int di = 0; di < 4; di++) { o_reg[qi][di] = 0.0f; } } // Strides for K and V const int kv_stride_batch = Nkv * S * D; const int kv_stride_head = S * D; int kv_base_offset = b * kv_stride_batch + hk * kv_stride_head; // Iterate over key tiles for (int k_tile = 0; k_tile < S; k_tile += BK) { int k_start = k_tile; // Load K and V tiles into smem for (int i = tid; i < BK * D_PAIRS; i += NUM_THREADS) { int k = i / D_PAIRS; int dp = i % D_PAIRS; int global_offset = kv_base_offset + (k_start + k) * D + dp * 2; __nv_bfloat162 k_val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(K + global_offset); __nv_bfloat162 v_val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(V + global_offset); k_smem[k * SMEM_STRIDE + dp] = k_val; v_smem[k * SMEM_STRIDE + dp] = v_val; } __syncthreads(); // Compute S = Q @ K^T for this warp's 8 queries // S_reg[qi][0] for key0, [1] for key1 float s_reg[8][2]; #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; qi++) { s_reg[qi][0] = 0.0f; s_reg[qi][1] = 0.0f; } // Tile over D in pairs #pragma unroll for (int dp = 0; dp < D_PAIRS; dp++) { // Load Q for 8 queries float q_vals[8][2]; // [qi][x,y] #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; qi++) { int q_idx = warp_id * 8 + qi; __nv_bfloat162 q_vec = q_smem[q_idx * SMEM_STRIDE + dp]; q_vals[qi][0] = __bfloat162float(q_vec.x); q_vals[qi][1] = __bfloat162float(q_vec.y); } // Load K for this thread's two keys __nv_bfloat162 k0_vec = k_smem[key0 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp]; __nv_bfloat162 k1_vec = k_smem[key1 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp]; float k0_0 = __bfloat162float(k0_vec.x); float k0_1 = __bfloat162float(k0_vec.y); float k1_0 = __bfloat162float(k1_vec.x); float k1_1 = __bfloat162float(k1_vec.y); // Accumulate #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; qi++) { float q0 = q_vals[qi][0]; float q1 = q_vals[qi][1]; s_reg[qi][0] += q0 * k0_0 + q1 * k0_1; s_reg[qi][1] += q0 * k1_0 + q1 * k1_1; } } // Apply scale (1/sqrt(D)) float scale = 1.0f / sqrtf((float)D); #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; qi++) { s_reg[qi][0] *= scale; s_reg[qi][1] *= scale; } // Online softmax and PV update #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; qi++) { float m_local = fmaxf(s_reg[qi][0], s_reg[qi][1]); // Warp reduce max m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 16)); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 8)); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 4)); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 2)); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 1)); float m_new = fmaxf(m_reg[qi], m_local); float exp_m_scale = expf(m_reg[qi] - m_new); m_reg[qi] = m_new; // Scale O #pragma unroll for (int di = 0; di < 4; di++) { o_reg[qi][di] *= exp_m_scale; } // Compute P and local sum float p0 = expf(s_reg[qi][0] - m_new); float p1 = expf(s_reg[qi][1] - m_new); float l_local = p0 + p1; l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 16); l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 8); l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 4); l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 2); l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 1); l_reg[qi] = l_reg[qi] * exp_m_scale + l_local; // Update O with P @ V // For this thread's d0..d3 // Need V[k0][d] and V[k1][d] for d in {d0, d0+1, d0+2, d0+3} #pragma unroll for (int di = 0; di < 4; di++) { int d_actual = d0 + di; int dp = d_actual >> 1; int is_y = d_actual & 1; float v0, v1; __nv_bfloat162 v0_vec = v_smem[key0 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp]; __nv_bfloat162 v1_vec = v_smem[key1 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp]; if (!is_y) { v0 = __bfloat162float(v0_vec.x); v1 = __bfloat162float(v1_vec.x); } else { v0 = __bfloat162float(v0_vec.y); v1 = __bfloat162float(v1_vec.y); } o_reg[qi][di] += p0 * v0 + p1 * v1; } } __syncthreads(); } // Finalize and write O #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; qi++) { int q_global = q_start_warp + qi; if (q_global >= S) continue; // safety, shouldn't happen float inv_l = 1.0f / l_reg[qi]; #pragma unroll for (int di = 0; di < 4; di++) { int d_actual = d0 + di; float o_val = o_reg[qi][di] * inv_l; int o_offset = b * q_stride_batch + hq * q_stride_head + q_global * D + d_actual; O[o_offset] = __float2bfloat16(o_val); } } } ''' # Then compile with appropriate flags ``` Wait, I need to be careful about writing single __nv_bfloat16 values to global memory. That's fine but not vectorized. We could write __nv_bfloat162 to do paired writes. But since each thread writes 4 consecutive values, and thread lane and lane+1 write adjacent pairs? Thread lane handles d=lane*4..lane*4+3. Thread lane writes to consecutive addresses, but within a warp, thread 0 writes to 0..3, thread 1 to 4..7. This is coalesced. Writing one by one is 4 separate stores. Better to use vectorized stores. Actually, writing 4 bytes at a time (one bf16) is not great for H100. We want 16-byte vectorized stores per thread if possible. Each thread has 4 bf16 values = 8 bytes. Two __nv_bfloat162 writes. Thread lane: d0 = lane*4. Writes at d0 and d0+2. d0 pair and d0+1 pair. We can form two __nv_bfloat162 values and write with two vectorized stores. But we need to be careful about alignment. d0 = lane*4. Global address = base + q_global*D + lane*4. This is divisible by 8 since lane*4 is divisible by 4, and base + q_global*128 is divisible by... if D=128, q_global*128 is divisible by 128. base alignment? The tensor is contiguous. Let me write the code carefully. ```python import torch import torch.nn as nn import os cuda_source = r''' #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <cuda_bf16.h> #include <math_constants.h> #include <cooperative_groups.h> using namespace cooperative_groups; #define D 128 #define BQ 64 #define BK 64 #define NUM_THREADS 256 #define D_PAIRS (D / 2) #define SMEM_STRIDE 65 __device__ __forceinline__ float2 bf1622float2(__nv_bfloat162 val) { float2 r; r.x = __bfloat162float(val.x); r.y = __bfloat162float(val.y); return r; } __global__ void gqa_attn_forward( const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ Q, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ K, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ V, __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ O, int B, int Nq, int Nkv, int S ) { int b = blockIdx.z; int hq = blockIdx.y; int q_tile = blockIdx.x; int num_head_groups = Nq / Nkv; int hk = hq / num_head_groups; int tid = threadIdx.x; int lane = tid & 31; int warp_id = tid >> 5; int q_start = q_tile * BQ; int q_warp_start = q_start + warp_id * 8; extern __shared__ char smem[]; __nv_bfloat162* q_smem = (__nv_bfloat162*)smem; __nv_bfloat162* k_smem = q_smem + BQ * SMEM_STRIDE; __nv_bfloat162* v_smem = k_smem + BK * SMEM_STRIDE; const int q_stride_batch = Nq * S * D; const int q_stride_head = S * D; const int kv_stride_batch = Nkv * S * D; const int kv_stride_head = S * D; int q_base_offset = b * q_stride_batch + hq * q_stride_head + q_start * D; // Load Q tile for (int i = tid; i < BQ * D_PAIRS; i += NUM_THREADS) { int q = i / D_PAIRS; int dp = i % D_PAIRS; __nv_bfloat162 val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(Q + q_base_offset + q * D + dp * 2); q_smem[q * SMEM_STRIDE + dp] = val; } __syncthreads(); // Each thread handles 2 keys int key0 = lane * 2; int key1 = lane * 2 + 1; // Each thread handles 4 D values: d0, d0+1, d0+2, d0+3 int d0 = lane * 4; int dp0 = lane * 2; // pair for d0, d0+1 int dp1 = lane * 2 + 1; ++di) { o_reg[qi][di] = 0.0f; } } int kv_base_offset = b * kv_stride_batch + hk * kv_stride_head; const float scale = 1.0f / sqrtf((float)D); for (int k_tile = 0; k_tile < S; k_tile += BK) { int k_start = k_tile; // Load K and V tiles for (int i = tid; i < BK * D_PAIRS; i += NUM_THREADS) { int k = i / D_PAIRS; int dp = i % D_PAIRS; int global_addr = kv_base_offset + (k_start + k) * D + dp * 2; __nv_bfloat162 k_val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(K + global_addr); qi < 8; ++qi) { s_reg[qi][0] = 0.0f; s_reg[qi][1] = 0.0f; } // Compute S tile #pragma unroll for (int dp = 0; dp < D_PAIRS; ++dp) { float q_vals[8][2]; #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { int q_idx = warp_id * 8 + qi; __nv_bfloat162 k1_vec = k_smem[key1 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp]; float2 k0_f = bf1622float2(k0_vec); float2 k1_f = bf1622float2(k1_vec); #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { s_reg[qi][0] += q_vals[qi][0] * k0_f.x + q_vals[qi][1] * k0_f.y; s_reg[qi][1] += q_vals[qi][0] * k1_f.x + q_vals[qi][1] * k1_f.y; } } #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { s_reg[qi][0] *= scale; s_reg[qi][1] *= scale; } // Softmax + PV #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { float m_local = fmaxf(s_reg[qi][0], s_reg[qi][1]); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 16)); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 8)); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 4)); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 2)); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 1)); float m_new = fmaxf(m_reg[qi], m_local); float exp_m = expf(m_reg[qi] - m_new); m_reg[qi] = m_new; #pragma unroll for (int di = 0; di < 4; ++di) { o_reg[qi][di] *= exp_m; } float p0 = expf(s_reg[qi][0] - m_new); float p1 = expf(s_reg[qi][1] - m_new); float l_local = p0 + p1; l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 16); l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 8); l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 4); l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 2); l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 1); l_reg[qi] = l_reg[qi] * exp_m + l_local; // Update O with PV #pragma unroll for (int di = 0; di < 4; ++di) { int d_actual = d0 + di; int dp = d_actual >> 1; bool is_y = d_actual & 1; __nv_bfloat162 v0_vec = v_smem[key0 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp]; __nv_bfloat162 v1_vec = v_smem[key1 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp]; float v0 = is_y ? __bfloat162float(v0_vec.y) : __bfloat162float(v0_vec.x); But `O` is `__nv_bfloat16*`, so pointer arithmetic is in elements. `lane*2` is element offset. `lane*2` is even. `__nv_bfloat162*` needs alignment to 2 elements = 4 bytes. `lane*2` is always an even number of elements, so `&O[o_base + lane*2]` is aligned to 4 bytes. Yes. Similarly `lane*2 + 64` is even. Good. Now for V reading in PV accumulation: ```cpp __nv_bfloat162 v0_1 = v_smem[key0 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp0]; __nv_bfloat162 v0_2 = v_smem[key0 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp1]; // v0_1.x = V[key0][lane*2], v0_1.y = V[key0][lane*2+1] // v0_2.x = V[key0][lane*2+64], v0_2.y = V[key0][lane*2+65] o_reg[qi][0] += p0 * __bfloat162float(v0_1.x); o_reg[qi][1] += p0 * __bfloat162float(v0_1.y); o_reg[qi][2] += p0 * __bfloat162float(v0_2.x); o_reg[qi][3] += p0 * __bfloat162float(v0_2.y); ``` Same for v1 with key1. Simplified kernel with BK=64 and key0=lane, key1=lane+32: ```cpp #include <torch/extension.h> #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <cuda_bf16.h> #include <math_constants.h> #include <cooperative_groups.h> using namespace cooperative_groups; #define D 128 #define BQ 64 #define BK 64 #define NUM_THREADS 256 #define D_PAIRS (D / 2) #define SMEM_STRIDE 65 __device__ __forceinline__ float2 bf1622float2(__nv_bfloat162 val) { float2 r; r.x = __bfloat162float(val.x); r.y = __bfloat162float(val.y); return r; } __global__ void gqa_attn_kernel( const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ Q, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ K, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ V, __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ O, int B, int Nq, int Nkv, int S ) { int b = blockIdx.z; int hq = blockIdx.y; int q_tile = blockIdx.x; int num_head_groups = Nq / Nkv; int hk = hq / num_head_groups; int tid = threadIdx.x; int lane = tid & 31; int warp_id = tid >> 5; int q_start = q_tile * BQ; int q_warp = q_start + warp_id * 8; extern __shared__ char smem[]; __nv_bfloat162* q_smem = (__nv_bfloat162*)smem; __nv_bfloat162* k_smem = q_smem + BQ * SMEM_STRIDE; __nv_bfloat162* v_smem = k_smem + BK * SMEM_STRIDE; const int q_stride_batch = Nq * S * D; const int q_stride_head = S * D; const int kv_stride_batch = Nkv * S * D; const int kv_stride_head = S * D; int q_base = b * q_stride_batch + hq * q_stride_head + q_start * D; // Load Q tile for (int i = tid; i < BQ * D_PAIRS; i += NUM_THREADS) { int q = i / D_PAIRS; int dp = i % D_PAIRS; __nv_bfloat162 val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(Q + q_base + q * D + dp * 2); q_smem[q * SMEM_STRIDE + dp] = val; } __syncthreads(); int key0 = lane; // keys 0..31 int key1 = lane + 32; // keys 32..63 int dp0 = lane; // D pairs: covers d = lane*2, lane*2+1 int dp1 = lane + 32; // D pairs: covers d = lane*2+64, lane*2+65 float o_reg[8][4]; float m_reg[8]; float l_reg[8]; #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { m_reg[qi] = -CUDART_INF_F; l_reg[qi] = 0.0f; #pragma unroll for (int di = 0; di < 4; ++di) { o_reg[qi][di] = 0.0f; } } int kv_base = b * kv_stride_batch + hk * kv_stride_head; const float scale = 1.0f / sqrtf((float)D); for (int k_tile = 0; k_tile < S; k_tile += BK) { int k_start = k_tile; // Load K and V for (int i = tid; i < BK * D_PAIRS; i += NUM_THREADS) { int k = i / D_PAIRS; int dp = i % D_PAIRS; int g = kv_base + (k_start + k) * D + dp * 2; __nv_bfloat162 k_val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(K + g); __nv_bfloat162 v_val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(V + g); k_smem[k * SMEM_STRIDE + dp] = k_val; v_smem[k * SMEM_STRIDE + dp] = v_val; } __syncthreads(); float s_reg[8][2]; #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { s_reg[qi][0] = 0.0f; s_reg[qi][1] = 0.0f; } // Compute S tile #pragma unroll for (int dp = 0; dp < D_PAIRS; ++dp) { float q_vals[8][2]; #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { int q_idx = warp_id * 8 + qi; __nv_bfloat162 qv = q_smem[q_idx * SMEM_STRIDE + dp]; q_vals[qi][0] = __bfloat162float(qv.x); q_vals[qi][1] = __bfloat162float(qv.y); } __nv_bfloat162 k0v = k_smem[key0 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp]; __nv_bfloat162 k1v = k_smem[key1 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp]; float2 k0f = bf1622float2(k0v); float2 k1f = bf1622float2(k1v); #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { s_reg[qi][0] += q_vals[qi][0] * k0f.x + q_vals[qi][1] * k0f.y; s_reg[qi][1] += q_vals[qi][0] * k1f.x + q_vals[qi][1] * k1f.y; } } #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { s_reg[qi][0] *= scale; s_reg[qi][1] *= scale; } // Softmax + PV #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { // Max float m_local = fmaxf(s_reg[qi][0], s_reg[qi][1]); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 16)); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 8)); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 4)); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 2)); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 1)); float m_new = fmaxf(m_reg[qi], m_local); float exp_m = expf(m_reg[qi] - m_new); m_reg[qi] = m_new; #pragma unroll for (int di = 0; di < 4; ++di) { o_reg[qi][di] *= exp_m; } float p0 = expf(s_reg[qi][0] - m_new); float p1 = expf(s_reg[qi][1] - m_new); float l_local = p0 + p1; l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 16); l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 8); l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 4); l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 2); l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 1); l_reg[qi] = l_reg[qi] * exp_m + l_local; // Load V for this thread's D dims and 2 keys __nv_bfloat162 v0_0 = v_smem[key0 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp0]; __nv_bfloat162 v0_1 = v_smem[key0 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp1]; __nv_bfloat162 v1_0 = v_smem[key1 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp0]; __nv_bfloat162 v1_1 = v_smem[key1 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp1]; float v00 = __bfloat162float(v0_0.x); float v01 = __bfloat162float(v0_0.y); float v02 = __bfloat162float(v0_1.x); float v03 = __bfloat162float(v0_1.y); float v10 = __bfloat162float(v1_0.x); float v11 = __bfloat162float(v1_0.y); float v12 = __bfloat162float(v1_1.x); float v13 = __bfloat162float(v1_1.y); o_reg[qi][0] += p0 * v00 + p1 * v10; o_reg[qi][1] += p0 * v01 + p1 * v11; o_reg[qi][2] += p0 * v02 + p1 * v12; o_reg[qi][3] += p0 * v03 + p1 * v13; } __syncthreads(); } // Write O #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { int q_global = q_warp + qi; if (q_global >= S) continue; float inv_l = 1.0f / l_reg[qi]; int o_base = b * q_stride_batch + hq * q_stride_head + q_global * D; __nv_bfloat162 o1, o2; o1.x = __float2bfloat16(o_reg[qi][0] * inv_l); o1.y = __float2bfloat16(o_reg[qi][1] * inv_l); o2.x = __float2bfloat16(o_reg[qi][2] * inv_l); o2.y = __float2bfloat16(o_reg[qi][3] * inv_l); *(__nv_bfloat162*)(O + o_base + lane * 2) = o1; *(__nv_bfloat162*)(O + o_base + lane * 2 + 64) = o2; } } ``` Wait, I notice `v0_0`, `v0_1`, etc. names might be confusing but it's fine. For each query in a tile: S_i = sum_d Q_i * K_j m = max(m, max_j S_j) P_j = exp(S_j - m) l_new = exp(m_old - m_new) * l_old + sum_j P_j O_new = exp(m_old - m_new) * O_old + sum_j P_j * V_j This is correct online softmax. So `expf` of non-positive numbers. max is 1.0. No overflow. Underflow to 0 is fine. This is the standard stable softmax. Wait, initial `m_reg = -INF`. For first tile, `m_new = max(-INF, m_tile) = m_tile`. `exp(-INF - m_tile) = 0`. But `exp_m` is `exp(m_reg[qi] - m_new)` where m_reg was -INF. This is `exp(-INF) = 0`. With dp0 = lane, key0 = lane. Bank = (lane + lane) % 32 = (2*lane) % 32. Lanes 0..15: even banks. Lanes 16..31: same as 0..15. So 2-way conflict for V reads across threads! Oh no! Code structure: ```python import torch import torch.nn as nn cuda_source = r''' #include <torch/extension.h> #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <cuda_bf16.h> #include <math_constants.h> #include <cooperative_groups.h> using namespace cooperative_groups; #define D 128 #define BQ 64 #define BK 64 #define NUM_THREADS 256 #define D_PAIRS (D / 2) #define SMEM_STRIDE 65 __device__ __forceinline__ float2 bf1622float2(__nv_bfloat162 val) { float2 r; r.x = __bfloat162float(val.x); r.y = __bfloat162float(val.y); return r; } __global__ void gqa_attn_kernel( const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ Q, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ K, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ V, __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ O, int B, int Nq, int Nkv, int S ) { int b = blockIdx.z; int hq = blockIdx.y; int q_tile = blockIdx.x; int num_head_groups = Nq / Nkv; int hk = hq / num_head_groups; int tid = threadIdx.x; int lane = tid & 31; int warp_id = tid >> 5; int q_start = q_tile * BQ; int q_warp = q_start + warp_id * 8; extern __shared__ char smem[]; __nv_bfloat162* q_smem = (__nv_bfloat162*)smem; __nv_bfloat162* k_smem = q_smem + BQ * SMEM_STRIDE; __nv_bfloat162* v_smem = k_smem + BK * SMEM_STRIDE; const int q_stride_batch = Nq * S * D; const int q_stride_head = S * D; const int kv_stride_batch = Nkv * S * D; const int kv_stride_head = S * D; int q_base = b * q_stride_batch + hq * q_stride_head + q_start * D; // Load Q tile #pragma unroll for (int i = tid; i < BQ * D_PAIRS; i += NUM_THREADS) { int q = i / D_PAIRS; int dp = i % D_PAIRS; __nv_bfloat162 val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(Q + q_base + q * D + dp * 2); q_smem[q * SMEM_STRIDE + dp] = val; } __syncthreads(); int key0 = lane; int key1 = lane + 32; int dp0 = lane * 2; int dp1 = lane * 2 + 1; float o_reg[8][4]; float m_reg[8]; float l_reg[8]; #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { m_reg[qi] = -CUDART_INF_F; l_reg[qi] = 0.0f; #pragma unroll for (int di = 0; di < 4; ++di) { o_reg[qi][di] = 0.0f; } } int kv_base = b * kv_stride_batch + hk * kv_stride_head; const float scale = 1.0f / sqrtf((float)D); for (int k_tile = 0; k_tile < S; k_tile += BK) { int k_start = k_tile; // Load K and V tiles #pragma unroll for (int i = tid; i < BK * D_PAIRS; i += NUM_THREADS) { int k = i / D_PAIRS; int dp = i % D_PAIRS; int g = kv_base + (k_start + k) * D + dp * 2; __nv_bfloat162 k_val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(K + g); __nv_bfloat162 v_val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(V + g); k_smem[k * SMEM_STRIDE + dp] = k_val; v_smem[k * SMEM_STRIDE + dp] = v_val; } __syncthreads(); float s_reg[8][2]; #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { s_reg[qi][0] = 0.0f; s_reg[qi][1] = 0.0f; } // Compute S tile #pragma unroll for (int dp = 0; dp < D_PAIRS; ++dp) { float q_vals[8][2]; #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { int q_idx = warp_id * 8 + qi; __nv_bfloat162 qv = q_smem[q_idx * SMEM_STRIDE + dp]; q_vals[qi][0] = __bfloat162float(qv.x); q_vals[qi][1] = __bfloat162float(qv.y); } __nv_bfloat162 k0v = k_smem[key0 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp]; __nv_bfloat162 k1v = k_smem[key1 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp]; float2 k0f = bf1622float2(k0v); float2 k1f = bf1622float2(k1v); #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { s_reg[qi][0] += q_vals[qi][0] * k0f.x + q_vals[qi][1] * k0f.y; s_reg[qi][1] += q_vals[qi][0] * k1f.x + q_vals[qi][1] * k1f.y; } } #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { s_reg[qi][0] *= scale; s_reg[qi][1] *= scale; } // Online softmax + PV update #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { float m_local = fmaxf(s_reg[qi][0], s_reg[qi][1]); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 16)); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 8)); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 4)); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 2)); m_local = fmaxf(m_local, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, m_local, 1)); float m_new = fmaxf(m_reg[qi], m_local); float exp_m = expf(m_reg[qi] - m_new); m_reg[qi] = m_new; #pragma unroll for (int di = 0; di < 4; ++di) { o_reg[qi][di] *= exp_m; } float p0 = expf(s_reg[qi][0] - m_new); float p1 = expf(s_reg[qi][1] - m_new); float l_local = p0 + p1; l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 16); l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 8); l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 4); l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 2); l_local += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, l_local, 1); l_reg[qi] = l_reg[qi] * exp_m + l_local; // Load V for this thread's D dims and 2 keys __nv_bfloat162 v0_0 = v_smem[key0 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp0]; __nv_bfloat162 v0_1 = v_smem[key0 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp1]; __nv_bfloat162 v1_0 = v_smem[key1 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp0]; __nv_bfloat162 v1_1 = v_smem[key1 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp1]; float v00 = __bfloat162float(v0_0.x); float v01 = __bfloat162float(v0_0.y); float v02 = __bfloat162float(v0_1.x); float v03 = __bfloat162float(v0_1.y); float v10 = __bfloat162float(v1_0.x); float v11 = __bfloat162float(v1_0.y); float v12 = __bfloat162float(v1_1.x); float v13 = __bfloat162float(v1_1.y); o_reg[qi][0] += p0 * v00 + p1 * v10; o_reg[qi][1] += p0 * v01 + p1 * v11; o_reg[qi][2] += p0 * v02 + p1 * v12; o_reg[qi][3] += p0 * v03 + p1 * v13; } __syncthreads(); } // Write O tile #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { int q_global = q_warp + qi; if (q_global >= S) continue; float inv_l = 1.0f / l_reg[qi]; int o_base = b * q_stride_batch + hq * q_stride_head + q_global * D; __nv_bfloat162 o1, o2; o1.x = __float2bfloat16(o_reg[qi][0] * inv_l); o1.y = __float2bfloat16(o_reg[qi][1] * inv_l); o2.x = __float2bfloat16(o_reg[qi][2] * inv_l); o2.y = __float2bfloat16(o_reg[qi][3] * inv_l); *(__nv_bfloat162*)(O + o_base + lane * 4) = o1; *(__nv_bfloat162*)(O + o_base + lane * 4 + 2) = o2; } } torch::Tensor gqa_attn_forward(torch::Tensor q, torch::Tensor k, torch::Tensor v) { auto q_contig = q.contiguous(); auto k_contig = k.contiguous(); auto v_contig = v.contiguous(); auto o = torch::empty_like(q_contig); int B = q_contig.size(0); int Nq = q_contig.size(1); int S = q_contig.size(2); int D_check = q_contig.size(3); int Nkv = k_contig.size(1); TORCH_CHECK(D_check == D); TORCH_CHECK(k_contig.size(2) == S); TORCH_CHECK(k_contig.size(3) == D_check); TORCH_CHECK(v_contig.sizes() == k_contig.sizes()); dim3 grid((S + BQ - 1) / BQ, Nq, B); dim3 block(NUM_THREADS); size_t smem_size = (BQ + BK + BK) * SMEM_STRIDE * sizeof(__nv_bfloat162); gqa_attn_kernel<<<grid, block, smem_size, at::cuda::getCurrentCUDAStream()>>>( reinterpret_cast<__nv_bfloat16*>(q_contig.data_ptr()), reinterpret_cast<__nv_bfloat16*>(k_contig.data_ptr()), reinterpret_cast<__nv_bfloat16*>(v_contig.data_ptr()), reinterpret_cast<__nv_bfloat16*>(o.data_ptr()), B, Nq, Nkv, S ); return o; } PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) { m.def("forward", &gqa_attn_forward, "Grouped Query Attention forward"); } ''' class ModelNew(nn.Module): def __init__(self, num_heads, num_kv_heads, head_dim): super().__init__() self.num_heads = num_heads self.num_kv_heads = num_kv_heads self.head_dim = head_dim self.gqa_cuda = torch.utils.cpp_extension.load_inline( name="gqa_attn_h100", cuda_sources=cuda_source, extra_cuda_cflags=[ "-O3", "-gencode", "arch=compute_90,code=sm_90", ], verbose=False, is_python_module=True, ) def forward(self, q, k, v): return self.gqa_cuda.forward(q, k, v) ``` Wait, `torch.utils.cpp_extension.load_inline` doesn't have an argument `is_python_module` in some versions. Issues to watch for: 1. `__nv_bfloat162` type: ensure `<cuda_bf16.h>` is included. 2. `__bfloat162float`: available on sm_80+. 3. `__float2bfloat16`: available. 4. `reinterpret_cast<__nv_bfloat16*>(tensor.data_ptr())`: data_ptr() returns void*. reinterpret_cast to __nv_bfloat16* is fine. One issue: `*(__nv_bfloat162*)(Q + q_base + q * D + dp * 2)`. We are dereferencing a misaligned pointer if `q_base + q*D + dp*2` is not 4-byte aligned. `q_base` is `b * stride_b + hq * stride_h + q_start * D`. Since D=128, `q_start*128` is divisible by 128. `hq * S * D` = `hq * 2048 * 128`. 2048*128 = 262144, divisible by 4. `b` term similarly. So `q_base` is divisible by any reasonable number. `q * D` = q * 128. Divisible by 4. `dp * 2` = even number. So the total offset is divisible by 2. For `__nv_bfloat162` alignment, we need 4-byte alignment. `offset * sizeof(bf16) = offset * 2`. So `q_base * 2 + q * 256 + dp * 4`. Since all terms are multiples of 4, the byte address is divisible by 4. Good. Similarly for K and V loads: `kv_base + (k_start + k) * D + dp * 2`. All divisible by 4. Good. Output writes: `o_base + lane*4`. Byte offset: `lane*4*2 = lane*8`. Divisible by 8. The second write `lane*4+2`: byte offset `(lane*4+2)*2 = lane*8 + 4`. Divisible by 4. If S is multiple of 64, all valid. Now, I want to verify the numerics once more for a single output element. O[b, hq, q, d] = sum_k softmax( Q[b,hq,q,:] @ K[b,hk,k,:] / sqrt(D) )_k * V[b,hk,k,d] Our kernel: For each key tile: S_k = dot(Q_q, K_k) / sqrt(D) m = max(m, max_k S_k) P_k = exp(S_k - m) l = l * exp(m_old - m) + sum_k P_k O += P_k * V_k (but scaled by exp(m_old - m) when m updates) At end: O /= l This is mathematically correct. One risk: FlashAttention might use block_size for reductions or have a different accumulation order, leading to slightly different floating point results. But online softmax accumulation order is standard. With bf16 inputs and fp32 accumulators, we should be within 1e-2 easily. Wait, there's another potential issue. In our kernel, we compute `expf(m_reg[qi] - m_new)`. When `m_reg` was -INF and `m_new` is a finite number, this is `expf(-INF)`. In CUDA, this is 0.0. But what if `m_reg` starts as `-CUDART_INF_F` which is `-inf_float`? `expf(-inf)` returns 0. Then `l_reg = 0 * 0 + l_local = l_local`. Correct. And `o_reg *= 0`. Since o_reg is 0, this is fine. Final check: are there any shared memory size limits I might have missed? 83KB per block is well below the 227KB limit for H100. And below 164KB for A100 too, if they compile for both. I'll compile for sm_90 and sm_80 to be safe if they test on A100, but the prompt says H100. I'll just do sm_90 primarily. Actually, adding sm_80 doesn't hurt. Wait, let me think about whether I should try to optimize register pressure further by using `#pragma unroll` carefully. `#pragma unroll` on the `qi` loops inside the `dp` loop might increase register pressure. But the compiler usually handles it well. I'll keep the unrolls. I also want to use `-maxrregcount=128` or similar? No, let compiler decide. One thing I want to add: `__launch_bounds__(NUM_THREADS)` on the kernel to help the compiler. It's a hint and often beneficial. ```cpp __launch_bounds__(NUM_THREADS) __global__ void gqa_attn_kernel(...) { ``` This tells the compiler max threads per block. Also, to further optimize for H100, we can try to enable more aggressive ILP by processing the 8 queries in a way that the FPUs are fully utilized. But the current code is likely okay. Another potential optimization: Instead of `for (int dp = 0; dp < D_PAIRS; ++dp)` loop, we can load `q_vals` for all 8 queries inside the loop, but since Q is in shared memory and each thread only needs one Q value at a time (broadcast), the compiler might not fully pipeline. Actually, since `q_vals` is an array of 16 floats per thread, it might consume registers. But it's overwritten each dp iteration. The compiler should keep it in registers. Similarly, `s_reg` is in registers. I think this kernel is good. Let me re-check if there's any `#include` missing for `CUDART_INF_F`. It's in `<math_constants.h>`. Included. One more thing: in the load loop for Q, K, V, should I add `#pragma unroll`? The compiler might not unroll a loop with 256-step. I'll leave it without `unroll` since the iteration count is large (16 for K/V, 16 for Q with 256 threads). The compiler knows best. Actually, for Q loading: `for (int i = tid; i < BQ * D_PAIRS; i += NUM_THREADS)`. BQ*D_PAIRS = 4096. NUM_THREADS=256. So 16 iterations. We can add `#pragma unroll 4` or leave it. I'll leave it. Similarly for K/V. Now, let me think about whether the output should be written with `__nv_bfloat162` properly. In the code: ```cpp *(__nv_bfloat162*)(O + o_base + lane * 4) = o1; ``` `O` is `__nv_bfloat16*`. `O + o_base + lane*4` is pointer arithmetic in `__nv_bfloat16` units. This address is 4-byte aligned. The write is to `__nv_bfloat162`. Correct. I think this is ready. Wait, but what if they use `torch.bfloat16` which maps to `at::BFloat16`? The ABI should match `__nv_bfloat16`. Let's trust it. I realize I should not output `BATCH = 4` etc. in the model code. The inputs are passed to `forward`. One final optimization: H100 supports FP8, but our problem uses bfloat16. So we stick to bf16. Actually, for H100, we can use `__nv_bfloat162` arithmetic? There is `__hmul2` and `__hfma2` for `__half2`, but for bfloat16, the math operations might promote to float anyway. Explicit float conversion is what we're doing. Let me produce the final code.
assistant```python import torch import torch.nn as nn cuda_source = r''' #include <torch/extension.h> #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <cuda_bf16.h> #include <math_constants.h> #include <cooperative_groups.h> #include <ATen/cuda/CUDAContext.h> using namespace cooperative_groups; #define D 128 #define BQ 64 #define BK 64 #define NUM_THREADS 256 #define D_PAIRS (D / 2) #define SMEM_STRIDE 65 __device__ __forceinline__ float2 bf1622float2(__nv_bfloat162 val) { float2 r; r.x = __bfloat162float(val.x); r.y = __bfloat162float(val.y); return r; } __launch_bounds__(NUM_THREADS) __global__ void gqa_attn_kernel( const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ Q, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ K, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ V, __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ O, int B, int Nq, int Nkv, int S ) { int b = blockIdx.z; int q_warp = q_start + warp_id * 8; extern __shared__ char smem[]; __nv_bfloat162* q_smem = (__nv_bfloat162*)smem; __nv_bfloat162* k_smem = q_smem + BQ * SMEM_STRIDE; __nv_bfloat162* v_smem = k_smem + BK * SMEM_STRIDE; // Load Q tile into shared memory (coalesced vectorized loads) for (int i = tid; i < BQ * D_PAIRS; i += NUM_THREADS) { int q = i / D_PAIRS; int dp = i % D_PAIRS; __nv_bfloat162 val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(Q + q_base + q * D + dp * 2); k_tile < S; k_tile += BK) { int k_start = k_tile; // Load K and V tiles into shared memory for (int i = tid; i < BK * D_PAIRS; i += NUM_THREADS) { int k = i / D_PAIRS; int dp = i % D_PAIRS; int g = kv_base + (k_start + k) * D + dp * 2; __nv_bfloat162 k_val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(K + g); __nv_bfloat162 v_val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(V + g); k_smem[k * SMEM_STRIDE + dp] = k_val; v_smem[k * SMEM_STRIDE + dp] = v_val; } __syncthreads(); float s_reg[8][2]; #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; qi < 8; ++qi) { s_reg[qi][0] = 0.0f; s_reg[qi][1] = 0.0f; } // Compute attention scores S = Q @ K^T for this tile #pragma unroll for (int dp = 0; dp < D_PAIRS; ++dp) { float q_vals[8][2]; } __nv_bfloat162 k0v = k_smem[key0 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp]; __nv_bfloat162 k1v = k_smem[key1 * SMEM_STRIDE + dp]; float2 k0f = bf1622float2(k0v); float2 k1f = bf1622float2(k1v); #pragma unroll for (int qi = 0; When we see a new score s that's larger than the current max m_reg: - exp_m = exp(m_reg - m_new) scales down all previous output values - l_reg is similarly scaled and then exp_s is added - m_reg is updated to m_new This should be correct for the running sum approach. Similarly for accesses with consecutive q but same dp: - q=0, dp=0: bank 0 - q=1, dp=0: bank 1 - etc. So shared memory access should be conflict-free. Here's the kernel design in detail: ```cpp #define D 128 #define BQ 8 // queries per block #define BK 64 // keys per tile #define NUM_THREADS 256 #define WARPS_PER_BLOCK 8 #define WARP_SIZE 32 __global__ void gqa_attn_kernel( const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ Q, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ K, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ V, __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ O, int B, int Nq, int Nkv, int S ) { int b = blockIdx.z; int hq = blockIdx.y; int q_tile = blockIdx.x; int tid = threadIdx.x; int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE; int lane = tid % WARP_SIZE; int num_head_groups = Nq / Nkv; int hk = hq / num_head_groups; // Each warp handles one query int q_local = warp_id; // 0..7 int q_global = q_tile * BQ + q_local; if (q_global >= S) return; // Load Q for this query: each thread loads 4 consecutive D elements int q_base = b * Nq * S * D + hq * S * D + q_global * D; float q_reg[4]; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { int d = lane * 4 + i; q_reg[i] = __bfloat162float(Q[q_base + d]); } // Shared memory for K and V tiles extern __shared__ char smem[]; __nv_bfloat16* k_smem = (__nv_bfloat16*)smem; __nv_bfloat16* v_smem = k_smem + BK * D; // Running softmax and output state float m_reg = -CUDART_INF_F; float l_reg = 0.0f; float o_reg[4]; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) o_reg[i] = 0.0f; int kv_base = b * Nkv * S * D + hk * S * D; float scale = 1.0f / sqrtf((float)D); // Process key/value tiles int num_tiles = (S + BK - 1) / BK; for (int tile = 0; tile < num_tiles; ++tile) { int k_start = tile * BK; int valid_keys = min(BK, S - k_start); // Load K tile (all 256 threads cooperate) for (int i = tid; i < BK * D; i += NUM_THREADS) { int k = i / D; int d = i % D; int g_idx = kv_base + (k_start + k) * D + d; k_smem[i] = (k < valid_keys) ? K[g_idx] : __float2bfloat16(0.0f); } // Load V tile for (int i = tid; i < BK * D; i += NUM_THREADS) { int k = i / D; int d = i % D; int g_idx = kv_base + (k_start + k) * D + d; v_smem[i] = (k < valid_keys) ? V[g_idx] : __float2bfloat16(0.0f); } __syncthreads(); // Each warp processes all keys in the tile for (int k = 0; k < valid_keys; ++k) { // Compute dot product Q · K[k] float partial = 0.0f; int k_offset = k * D + lane * 4; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { float k_val = __bfloat162float(k_smem[k_offset + i]); partial += q_reg[i] * k_val; } // Warp reduce to get full score partial += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, partial, 16); partial += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, partial, 8); partial += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, partial, 4); partial += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, partial, 2); partial += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, partial, 1); float score = partial * scale; // Online softmax float m_new = fmaxf(m_reg, score); float exp_m = expf(m_reg - m_new); float exp_s = expf(score - m_new); l_reg = l_reg * exp_m + exp_s; m_reg = m_new; // Load V and accumulate weighted sum int v_offset = k * D + lane * 4; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { o_reg[i] *= exp_m; float v_val = __bfloat162float(v_smem[v_offset + i]); o_reg[i] += exp_s * v_val; } } __syncthreads(); } // Write output float inv_l = 1.0f / l_reg; int o_base = b * Nq * S * D + hq * S * D + q_global * D; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { int d = lane * 4 + i; O[o_base + d] = __float2bfloat16(o_reg[i] * inv_l); } } ``` This looks correct and clean. Let me check shared memory: - K tile: 64 * 128 * 2 = 16384 bytes - V tile: 64 * 128 * 2 = 16384 bytes - Total: 32768 bytes = 32 KB - H100 can handle up to 227 KB per block, and 228 KB per SM. So 32 KB is very comfortable. Thread counts: 256 threads per block, each warp handles 1 query. 8 warps handle 8 queries. For grid: ((S + BQ - 1) / BQ, Nq, B) = (256, 32, 4) for S=2048. Total blocks = 32768. Occupancy: 256 threads * 32 blocks/SM max = 8192 threads, but H100 only supports 2048 threads/SM. So max 8 blocks per SM at 256 threads each. Total concurrent blocks = 144 * 8 = 1152. Waves = 32768 / 1152 ≈ 28.5. This should perform reasonably well. And correctness is straightforward to verify. Actually, I can optimize the K/V loading by loading as __nv_bfloat162 pairs instead of individual elements: ```cpp __nv_bfloat162* k_smem2 = (__nv_bfloat162*)smem; __nv_bfloat162* v_smem2 = k_smem2 + BK * D / 2; for (int i = tid; i < BK * D / 2; i += NUM_THREADS) { ... k_smem2[i] = ... } ``` This halves the number of loads. But I need to be careful with the V access pattern later. Let me also check: can I avoid syncthreads between K and V loads? In my current code, I load both K and V, then __syncthreads. I could interleave them or just load both in one loop. But keeping them separate is fine. Actually, one optimization: I can compute scores for key k while loading V for key k+1, but that complicates the code. Let me keep it simple first. Let me also think about register usage: - q_reg: 4 floats - o_reg: 4 floats - m_reg: 1 float - l_reg: 1 float - partial, score, m_new, exp_m, exp_s: ~5 floats - k_val, v_val: ~2 floats - Total: ~17 floats = 17 registers - Plus loop counters, indices: ~5-10 registers - Total: ~25-30 registers per thread - For 256 threads: 6400-7680 registers per block - H100 limit: 64K registers per SM = 65536 - Max blocks per SM: floor(65536 / 7680) = 8 - But thread limit: 2048 / 256 = 8 blocks - So we're limited by threads, not registers. Good! Let me write this kernel now.

In the loop over k: ```cpp for (int k = 0; k < valid_keys; ++k) { float partial = 0.0f; int k_offset = k * D + lane * 4; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { float k_val = __bfloat162float(k_smem[k_offset + i]); partial += q_reg[i] * k_val; } // Warp reduce partial += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, partial, 16); partial += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, partial, 8); partial += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, partial, 4); partial += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, partial, 2); partial += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, partial, 1); float score = partial * scale; ``` ALL threads in the warp compute the same `score`. Then: ```cpp float m_new = fmaxf(m_reg, score); float exp_m = expf(m_reg - m_new); float exp_s = expf(score - m_new); l_reg = l_reg * exp_m + exp_s; m_reg = m_new; ``` ALL threads in the warp update m_reg, l_reg identically. K[g_idx] : __float2bfloat16(0.0f); } ``` But `i` goes from `tid` to `BK * D` with step `NUM_THREADS`. For BK=64, D=128: - Total elements = 64 * 128 = 8192 - NUM_THREADS = 256 - Each thread handles 8192 / 256 = 32 elements So thread 0 handles elements 0, 256, 512, ..., 7936 Thread 0 computes: - k = 0/128 = 0, d = 0; k=2, d=0; k=4, d=0; etc. Wait, 256 = 2 * 128, so: - i=0: k=0, d=0 - i=256: k=2, d=0 - i=512: k=4, d=0 Thread 1 handles i=1, 257, 513, ... - i=1: k=0, d=1 - i=257: k=2, d=1 So thread N handles elements where d = N % 128, but since the step is 256 = 2*128, and 128 doesn't divide 256 evenly... wait, 256 / 128 = 2, so: - i = tid + j * 256 - k = i / 128 = (tid + j * 256) / 128 = tid/128 + 2j - d = (tid + j * 256) % 128 = tid % 128 So for thread tid: - d = tid (if tid < 128) or tid - 128 (if tid >= 128) - k increments by 2 each time So thread 0 handles d=0 for all even k Thread 1 handles d=1 for all even k ... Thread 127 handles d=127 for all even k Thread 128 handles d=0 for all odd k Thread 129 handles d=1 for all odd k ... This covers all k and d! Good. The indexing is correct. This would be slow but correct, and I can use it to verify my understanding. ```cpp __global__ void naive_attn(...) { int b = blockIdx.z; int hq = blockIdx.y; int q = blockIdx.x; int d = threadIdx.x; if (d >= D) return; int num_head_groups = Nq / Nkv; int hk = hq / num_head_groups; // Load Q[d] int q_base = b * Nq * S * D + hq * S * D + q * D; float qd = __bfloat162float(Q[q_base + d]); int kv_base = b * Nkv * S * D + hk * S * D; float scale = 1.0f / sqrtf((float)D); // Compute all scores float m = -CUDART_INF_F; for (int k = 0; k < S; ++k) { float dot = 0.0f; for (int dd = 0; dd < D; ++dd) { float qdd = __bfloat162float(Q[q_base + dd]); float kdd = __bfloat162float(K[kv_base + k * D + dd]); dot += qdd * kdd; } float score = dot * scale; m = fmaxf(m, score); } float l = 0.0f; for (int k = 0; k < S; ++k) { float dot = 0.0f; for (int dd = 0; dd < D; ++dd) { float qdd = __bfloat162float(Q[q_base + dd]); float kdd = __bfloat162float(K[kv_base + k * D + dd]); dot += qdd * kdd; } float score = dot * scale; l += expf(score - m); } float o = 0.0f; for (int k = 0; k < S; ++k) { float dot = 0.0f; for (int dd = 0; dd < D; ++dd) { float qdd = __bfloat162float(Q[q_base + dd]); float kdd = __bfloat162float(K[kv_base + k * D + dd]); dot += qdd * kdd; } float score = dot * scale; float p = expf(score - m) / l; float vd = __bfloat162float(V[kv_base + k * D + d]); o += p * vd; } int o_base = b * Nq * S * D + hq * S * D + q * D; O[o_base + d] = __float2bfloat16(o); } ``` This is O(S^2 * D^2) and would be incredibly slow, but if it's correct, then I know the formula is right. Actually, for __nv_bfloat162, 64 columns means each row is 256 bytes = 64 banks (assuming 4-byte banks). Accessing a __nv_bfloat162 from consecutive threads accesses consecutive 4-byte words, mapping to consecutive banks. With 32 threads in a warp, we access banks 0-31. Then lane+32 accesses banks 32-63. This would avoid bank conflicts since each thread in a warp accesses a different bank! Wait, let me think about shared memory bank layout. On modern GPUs (Ampere and later), shared memory has 32 banks of 4 bytes each (for 32-bit mode) or 32 banks of 8 bytes (for 64-bit mode). Actually, for bfloat16: - Bfloat16 is 2 bytes. - __nv_bfloat162 is 4 bytes. If I layout as `__nv_bfloat162 smem[N][64]`: - Thread lane accesses `smem[row][lane]`: consecutive lanes access consecutive 4-byte words, mapping to banks based on the word address. - With 32 banks of 4 bytes, word at offset 0 -> bank 0, offset 4 -> bank 1, etc. - So lane 0 accesses bank 0, lane 1 accesses bank 1, ..., lane 31 accesses bank 31. - All threads in a warp access different banks -> no bank conflicts! Then for the second half (lane+32), lane 32 accesses bank 0 again (since 32 * 4 = 128 bytes, and 128 % 128 = 0 with 32 banks of 4 bytes). Wait, let me recalculate. Shared memory address = base + row * stride + col * sizeof(type). Bank = (address / 4) % 32 for 4-byte banks. For stride=64 and type=__nv_bfloat162 (4 bytes): address = base + row * 256 + col * 4 bank = (address / 4) % 32 = (base/4 + row * 64 + col) % 32 For lane=0: bank = (base/4 + row*64 + 0) % 32 For lane=1: bank = (base/4 + row*64 + 1) % 32 ... For lane=31: bank = (base/4 + row*64 + 31) % 32 For lane=32: bank = (base/4 + row*64 + 32) % 32 = (base/4 + row*64 + 0) % 32 So lanes 0 and 32 access the same bank! This means if a warp reads both lanes 0 and 32 simultaneously, there will be a 2-way bank conflict. With SMEM_STRIDE=65: address = base + row * 260 + col * 4 bank = (base/4 + row * 65 + col) % 32 For lane=0: bank = (base/4 + row*65 + 0) % 32 For lane=32: bank = (base/4 + row*65 + 32) % 32 = (base/4 + row*65 + 0) % 32 (since 32 % 32 = 0) Wait, that's the same issue unless 65 mod 32 introduces a shift. 65 % 32 = 1. So for consecutive rows, the starting bank shifts by 1. For lane=0: bank = (base/4 + row*65) % 32 For lane=32: bank = (base/4 + row*65 + 32) % 32 = (base/4 + row*65) % 32 Still the same! Lanes 0 and 32 have the same bank. But actually, in the first kernel, I had 8 warps and each warp only processes 32 keys through lane (0-31). There wasn't a scenario where a single warp tried to access both lane 0 and lane 32, because lane 32 doesn't exist in a warp! Wait, but bank conflicts happen when multiple threads in the same warp try to access different data in the SAME bank. For key0 access: lane i accesses row i. All different rows, so no conflicts regardless of bank. For key1 access: lane i accesses row i+32. All different rows, so no conflicts. Actually, the bank conflict would only occur if two threads in the same warp, accessing the SAME row, read different banks. Here each thread accesses a different row, so there's no conflict. In the load loops: ```cpp for (int i = tid; i < BQ * D_PAIRS; i += NUM_THREADS) { int q = i / D_PAIRS; int dp = i % D_PAIRS; q_smem[q * SMEM_STRIDE + dp] = val; } ``` With 256 threads: - Thread 0: q=0, dp=0; thread 1: q=0, dp=1; ... thread 31: q=0, dp=31; thread 32: q=1, dp=0... Actually, wait, is 48KB the inclusive limit? CUDA documentation says: "For devices of compute capability 3.x, the maximum amount of shared memory per thread block is 48 KB. One more check: in the output write: ```cpp *(__nv_bfloat162*)(O + o_base + lane * 4) = o1; *(__nv_bfloat162*)(O + o_base + lane * 4 + 2) = o2; ``` o_base + lane * 4 + 2 needs to be 4-byte aligned for __nv_bfloat162. Since lane * 4 is a multiple of 4, lane * 4 + 2 is a multiple of 2 but not 4. __nv_bfloat162 requires 4-byte alignment! lane * 4 + 2 = 2, 6, 10, ... These are NOT divisible by 4! OH MY GOD. This causes undefined behavior on GPUs! Actually, for `__nv_bfloat162`, the alignment requirement is 4 bytes (since it's a 4-byte type). Writing to an unaligned address can cause incorrect results or even exceptions. Let me verify: - o_base is aligned to at least 2 bytes (since it's a bfloat16 pointer).
